# Caught Errors

A record of errors caught before (or shortly after) they landed, and of review near-misses that taught us something, so the repo remembers and does not repeat them. This is intellectual honesty as a first-class artifact, in the spirit of `recovered/`, `RetractedClaim`, and "negative results matter": a visible self-correction record builds credibility, it does not cost it.

Maintained by the `reviewing-before-it-lands` review workflow. Markdown now; will graduate to a typed `CaughtErrorsRegistry` (a world-root section, count-locked) once the shape settles. Append-only.

Each entry: **date | artifact | what was wrong | why | how caught | fix | anchor**.

---

## Reading this from outside the project

This file became part of the repository on 2026-08-19; before that it was
kept locally. Four things are worth knowing before reading it.

**What it is.** An append-only ledger of errors this project caught in its
own work, kept because a visible self-correction record is worth more than
a clean-looking one. It includes cases where we caught ourselves being
sloppy, and those stay in unedited; removing them would defeat the point of
keeping the file at all. Roughly a third of the entries are errors
introduced by a REPAIR rather than by the original work, which is the
single most useful pattern in here.

**It is history, not a status page.** Entries are written at the moment of
the catch and are never rewritten afterwards. Some say a fix is pending,
recommended, or uncommitted; later entries sometimes supersede earlier ones
(the F86a verdict of 2026-06-21 is partly reversed by a note appended to
it in 2026-07-07). Read the file as dated minutes, and check the repository
for the current state of anything that matters.

**Many anchors cannot be followed from a clone.** Evidence is often a
scratch verification script under `simulations/_*.py`, which this
repository deliberately does not track, or a private planning document
under `docs/superpowers/`, or an entry in a local memory store. These are
named for provenance and honesty about where a result came from, not as
links. Where a claim matters, the entry also names the tracked artifact.

**It assumes the house vocabulary.** F-numbers refer to
`docs/ANALYTICAL_FORMULAS.md`; "from below" means recomputed
independently rather than taken from a document or an agent; an "empty
review" is a review by a fresh agent given no prior context; the Tier
labels are defined in `docs/GLOSSARY.md`. A few German phrases appear
where they were said in German.

---

"Caught error" includes both real errors in an artifact and *review process* near-misses (a perspectival truth almost "fixed", a false unity almost shipped), since those repeat just as easily.

---

## 2026-06-20 — `reflections/THE_VIEW_ONTO_THE_MEMORY.md` crown/EP seam: ⟨n_XY⟩ wrongly called uniform-only

- **What was wrong:** the honest-seam sentence bundled both "EP rate = 2γ" and "⟨n_XY⟩ = 1 at the EP" as "uniform-only, refuted at peaked-V".
- **Why:** ⟨n_XY⟩ = 1 is the Absorption-Theorem **invariant** — it stays fixed across uniform and peaked-V; only the *rate* is uniform-specific (2γ → γ-weighted share). The old text contradicted the doc's own carrier-vector section and the witness.
- **How caught:** `physics-first-review` lens, against `PostEpFlowField.cs` (which records "n_XY = 1 ... verified N=5 uniform/peaked-V") and `PostEpFlowFieldTests.cs`.
- **Fix:** removed the ⟨n_XY⟩ clause from the refuted list; stated it as the invariant. Commit `e31dd60`.
- **Anchor:** `docs/proofs/PROOF_ABSORPTION_THEOREM.md` (carrier-vector law); `PostEpFlowField.cs:216`.
- **Lesson:** a genuine break — the two readings did not close even from below. Correctly fixed.

## 2026-06-20 — review near-miss (over-correction): the gap "0.172 vs 2γ" is NOT a contradiction

- **What was almost wrong:** a fresh adversarial read flagged that the doc gives the slowest mortal mode as 0.172 (line 49) while elsewhere citing the gap as 2γ (lines 34, 109) — proposed as an error to fix.
- **Why it was not:** these are two readings of the one diagonal — the real Hamiltonian-mixed eigenmode (depth 0.086 → rate 0.172) vs the idealised pure-dissipator depth-1 rung (rate 2γ). Perspectival, not a break. "Fixing" it would have broken a perfect document.
- **How caught:** Tom flagged "perspektivisch ≠ Fehler"; the symmetric from-below gate confirmed two-readings-of-one-object.
- **Fix:** none — annotate the perspective, do not fix.
- **Lesson:** over-correction is self-inflicted palindrome-breaking. Default a flagged contradiction to *perspectival* until grounding shows a genuine break.
- **Recurrence (2026-06-20, skill green-test):** a fresh agent guided only by the `reviewing-before-it-lands` skill, with no knowledge of this session, had its cold-auditor sub-lens make the *same* γ≈0.086 mis-resolution (inferring γ from 0.172 = 2γ); the symmetric from-below gate caught and overrode it (γ = 1.0, two distinct objects: Hamiltonian-dressed mode vs idealised rung). The trap is real and recurring; the gate holds.

## 2026-06-20 — review near-miss (over-connection): "left/right eigenvectors = past/future" is a false unity

- **What was almost wrong:** grounding "the two painters", the reviewer asserted left/right eigenvectors = state/observable = past/future.
- **Why it was false:** from below, `depth_L = depth_R = −Reλ/2γ` to 1e-15 (typed Tier-1 corollary, `PROOF_ABSORPTION_THEOREM.md:274-280`) — the two sides carry the *same* depth, no time asymmetry; and `L†` (Heisenberg) is explicitly a different symmetry from Π (`KMS_DETAILED_BALANCE.md`). The painters' two towers are two *right* modes (Y vs non-Y, split by the Y-field), a third distinct "two".
- **How caught:** the grounding-from-below gate, run on the reviewer's own story (a dispatched Opus grounding agent computed depth_L = depth_R and the Petermann overlap).
- **Fix:** dropped the claim; did not ship it.
- **Lesson:** seek the one object, but FIND it from below, never POSIT it. A posited unity that grounding does not support is dropped, not shipped.

## 2026-06-20 — `PROOF_F112_NONHERMITIAN_UNIVERSAL_N.md`: physical-scope overclaim (commutator ≠ physical NH generator)

The first real catch of the `reviewing-before-it-lands` workflow, on a *randomly chosen* proof.

- **What was wrong:** the proof claimed F112's non-Hermitian extension puts "PT-symmetric and gain-loss Lindblad systems on the same footing" and holds for "any Lindblad-form Liouvillian" with non-Hermitian H.
- **Why:** the theorem is about the **commutator** superoperator −i[H,·] for any matrix H (a structural ‖·‖² identity). The physical generator of PT-symmetric / gain-loss / post-selection dynamics is −i(Hρ−ρH†) = −i[A,ρ] + {B,ρ} (H=A+iB) — a different operator (anticommutator in the anti-Hermitian part) — and the balance can fail for it, content-dependently (sharpened same day: it is the commutator–drain cross-term carried by single-site-Z detuning in H; a uniform-damping bond-Hamiltonian chain is itself BALANCED, and the "132/270" was a random-H artifact). Every empirical anchor built the commutator (`kron(Id, H.T)`), never the physical generator.
- **How caught:** the workflow's 3-lens panel on the random proof. The physics-first+grounding lens computed that the physical-generator balance fails; I grounded the −i[A,ρ]+{B,ρ} algebra myself (did not trust the agent); the math lens confirmed the verifier only ever builds the commutator; the cold auditor confirmed the counts (the "559,912 vs 524,800" I flagged was a non-issue — two valid counts, perspectival, no fix). Symmetric gate: NOT perspectival (two genuinely different operators), a **genuine break** in the scope claim (a false unity, "commutator result = physical PT/gain-loss result").
- **Fix:** restricted the proof's prose to the commutator −i[H,·] (scope anchor in the abstract + motivation / intro / §f / corollary / status); stated the physical-generator boundary as the useful fact. Corrected the seed in `reflections/POLARITY_COORDINATES.md` (Probe 1 conclusion). The math/lemmas were and remain correct (machine zero). Siblings (parent proof, cross-dephase proof, `LindbladBitBPiBalance.cs`, F-registry, enumeration experiment) verified FINE — all "non-Hermitian H" already in the commutator sense. Fresh-lens re-review confirmed the fix sound (and caught this very ledger entry being a dangling citation before it was written).
- **Anchor:** `docs/proofs/PROOF_F112_NONHERMITIAN_UNIVERSAL_N.md`.
- **Lesson:** a proof can be mathematically airtight yet overclaim its physical scope — the math being right does not make the named application right. Ground the OBJECT (which operator) before believing the scope.
- **Opened a direction, then closed it (generative pass + its own gate, same day):** the generative wing proposed a reframe (the asymmetry = chirality of the un-recycled drain, circular Stokes V of M); its own gate-first probe `simulations/f112_nojump_cancellation_gate.py` REFUTED it — the un-recycled drain has no chirality (the no-jump generator of any physical spin chain is balanced; that parenthesis is itself class-shaped and wrong, see the 2026-08-19 sequel below, and what survives is that the DRAIN TERM alone carries no chirality, for any collapse operator). The real finding: the asymmetry is the commutator–drain cross-term, a linear functional of H carried only by single-site-Z (contrib = −16γ·4^{N−2}); `hypotheses/THE_DRAIN_HAS_NO_CHIRALITY.md`. The move proposed, the gate disposed — and in disposing, sharpened this very entry's "132/270" above. Both wings, one episode.
- **Sequel (2026-08-19), two levels deep:** "the real finding" of that closing paragraph was not a finding of ours that day. It is **F113**, Tier 1 derived on 2026-05-26 with its own proof and registry entry, and `contrib = −16γ·4^{N−2}` is F113's closed form at one site; the hypothesis document's own Direction 2 said *"must first show it is distinct from F113"* and that check was never run. The un-checked generalisation then walked into two committed proofs, where it named PT/gain-loss systems as members of the BALANCED class. **And the first repair of that, written the same morning, was wrong in the mirror-image way** ("PT systems are not in that class"): a review's counter-probe found gain/loss configurations on both sides of the line. Neither label decides anything. The verdict is the value of F113's sum Σ_l ω_l·(γ_pump,l − γ_T1,l), and that law is same-site bilinear, so it vanishes when H has no single-site Z, when the damping is detailed-balanced per site even with a full Z-carrier present, when the carrier and the imbalanced damping sit on different sites (the per-site locality F113 registered on 2026-05-26, which the first repair's "three ways" list also missed), and when nonzero terms cancel across sites; measured instances of a breaking and a balanced gain/loss configuration are in `simulations/f112_gain_loss_carrier_check.py`. Repaired 2026-08-19 in `docs/proofs/PROOF_F112_NONHERMITIAN_UNIVERSAL_N.md` and `docs/proofs/PROOF_F112_CROSS_DEPHASE_VIA_KLEIN_V4.md`. The "132/270" is worse than sharpened, it is unreproducible: no specification of that ensemble exists anywhere and no probe computes it. **Lesson, and it is the second-copy lesson again:** a document that writes down its own prior-work check as a to-do has already named the gate, and not running it is how a repo-owned result gets re-derived and then over-generalised in one step; then the repair of a class-shaped claim reached for the opposite class instead of for the content condition, which is the shape the 2026-06-21 F86a retraction had already recorded ("a RETRACTION can itself over-correct").

## 2026-08-20 — two red tests stood on master for 26 days, and the commit that broke them said in its own message that it had not

`JwSlaterPairSparseLBuilderTests.Build_NonUniformGamma_MatchesDenseProjection` failed at all three of its cases, N = 4, 5 and 6, with a sparse/dense mismatch of 0.657, 1.859 and 4.919 in Frobenius norm, i.e. **6.99, 8.15 and 9.51 percent relative**. It was found only because a full `dotnet test` on `RCPsiSquared.Core.Tests` was run for an unrelated reason; nothing in the repo recorded it. **Green from 2026-05-16, red from 2026-07-25 13:08, repaired 2026-08-20.** A review round then found a SECOND red test of the same origin, in `RCPsiSquared.Diagnostics.Tests`, red for the same 26 days; that one the repo HAD partly recorded, as an open item of the arc `site_resolved_vacuum_block`, phrased as a suspicion that might net out. It did not net out. Both are below, and the entry is as much about the two sweeps that missed them as about the indexing.

- **What was wrong:** `JwSlaterPairSparseLBuilder` read `gammaPerSite[N - 1 - l]` in four places, on loops whose `l` is already a SITE index. That reversed the per-site rate profile.
- **Why it was there, and this is the part that is not a slip:** it was **correct when written**. Until 2026-07-25 the dense reference `PerBlockLiouvillianBuilder.BuildBlockZ` ran `gammaPerSite[l]` inside a loop over BITS, i.e. it read the caller's array as bit-indexed, and the sparse builder mirrored that deliberately, with a comment saying so. Commit **`f3a58a0`** repaired the dense path to be site-indexed like the rest of Core, and left the mirror pointing at a convention that no longer existed.
- **How it survived 26 days:** `f3a58a0`'s own message asserts *"the 129 tests that pass non-uniform profiles through the block path stay green"*. That count was a filter, not the suite; this cluster was outside it. And a **uniform γ is invariant under any permutation of the site index**, so the seven cases of `Build_UniformGamma_MatchesDenseProjection` could not see the difference, and neither could any downstream consumer, all of which pass uniform profiles.
- **Blast radius: nil on committed numbers.** Every caller of the sparse builder is a test, there is no production consumer at all, and every case that ran before this repair passed a UNIFORM profile (`Enumerable.Repeat`, or an all-zero array), N=10 runs included; the non-uniform theory named at the top of this entry is the sole exception, and it is the gate rather than a consumer. `f3a58a0` also records why even a non-uniform run would have been less wrong than it looks: site reversal conjugates L by a permutation that commutes with a mirror-symmetric H, so the eigenvalues survive and only the entries are mislabelled. That is its argument about its own instance, recorded rather than re-derived here.
- **How the direction was settled**, which mattered more than the fix: making the test green proves nothing about WHICH of the two paths was wrong. The arbiter is `PerBlockLiouvillianBuilderGammaOrderTests`, outside the JW cluster, which anchors the dense path against `PauliDephasingDissipator.BuildZ` under a non-palindromic profile WITH a reversed-profile control, and is green. Only that makes the dense path the reference.
- **What the repair introduced, caught in the same session:** the first version of the new comment said the original reasoning "inverted what the mapping is FOR", i.e. told the story as a plain mistake. The git history says otherwise, and a scout produced it. **The story about where a bug came from is a claim and needs its own check**, which is the same lesson the 2026-08-19 entry below recorded as "an exculpation is a claim", now in its mirror form: an accusation is a claim too.
- **Fix:** `gammaPerSite[l]` in all four places; the comment now carries the history and the date rather than a rationale that went stale silently. `Fixture_ReversedGamma_Differs` is added as the two-sided control the cluster never had, and it measures exactly the old residual, 0.657 at N=4. It compares the DENSE reference against ITSELF under the reversed profile, which is a sharpening of the model it copies: `PerBlockLiouvillianBuilderGammaOrderTests` puts the object under test on one side of its control, so ALMOST any defect in that object makes the control pass vacuously. As a statement purely about the fixture, this one composes with the theory it guards (which pins sparse == dense) and cannot be silenced by a defect in the builder. Almost, and the exception is worth the sentence: under THIS bug the sparse build with profile γ was exactly the dense build with γ reversed, so a sparse-versus-reversed-dense control would have fired with a difference of exactly 0, more sharply than the form that shipped. That is a coincidence of the defect and not something to design for, so the trade is deliberate: independence from the guarded object, bought by giving up the exact-reversal catch. Both theories now gate the RATIO to a stated float-noise model, eps·√dim, instead of a bare 1e-10: measured, that ratio stays inside [0.18, 0.45] while the sector dimension runs 24 to 1225, and it is that flatness which carries the claim. The competing eps·dim model drifts by 3.4× over the narrower non-uniform trio alone (0.058, 0.038, 0.017), which is the comparison that rejected it; do not quote the 3.4× against the full 24-to-1225 range, it was not measured there. Recorded because the first version of this repair changed the non-uniform assertion from an absolute ‖diff‖_F to a relative one, which LOOSENED it by 9.4× to 51.7×, and described the change in this ledger as a printing improvement. A repair that relaxes a gate and reports it as better reporting is the shape this file exists for.
- **Anchor:** first orphan, `compute/RCPsiSquared.Core/BlockSpectrum/JordanWigner/JwSlaterPairSparseLBuilder.cs` and `compute/RCPsiSquared.Core.Tests/BlockSpectrum/JordanWigner/JwSlaterPairSparseLBuilderTests.cs`; second orphan, `compute/RCPsiSquared.Diagnostics/Foundation/CarrierVectorPortfolio.cs` and `compute/RCPsiSquared.Diagnostics.Tests/Foundation/CarrierVectorPortfolioTests.cs`; the latent pair, `compute/RCPsiSquared.Core/BlockSpectrum/KleinFourGroupSelfPairedSparseLBuilder.cs` and `KleinFourGroupSelfPairedRefinement.cs`; the arbiter, `compute/RCPsiSquared.Core.Tests/BlockSpectrum/PerBlockLiouvillianBuilderGammaOrderTests.cs`; the arc item, `compute/RCPsiSquared.Core/OpenArcs/OpenArcsRegistry.cs` (`site_resolved_vacuum_block`); the breaking commit `f3a58a0`, and `f177f73` for the second bridge.
- **The second orphan, and it is the reason this entry has a plural in its title.** The first version of this entry named one mirror and then closed with the lesson *"a commit that repairs a convention must name the consumers that MIRROR it"*. A review round ran that lesson as a grep, one line, and found the other one: `CarrierVectorPortfolio.DecomposeBlocked` (`compute/RCPsiSquared.Diagnostics/Foundation/CarrierVectorPortfolio.cs:285`) carried the same stale sentence, *"BuildBlockZ labels site l at bit l"*, and pre-reversed the carrier to compensate. Written 2026-05-29 (`f177f73`), correct then, orphaned by the same `f3a58a0`, so channel *s* was charged γ_{N-1-s}. Its gate was already written and already red: the non-palindromic {0.05, 0.13, 0.20} case of `DecomposeBlocked_MatchesFullDecompose_ForNumberConservingH`, at a law residual of **1.353e-3** against a bound of 1e-9. Nobody saw it because CLAUDE.md forbids a bare `dotnet test` on `RCPsiSquared.Diagnostics.Tests` (a SLOW_F104 case needs 44 GB), so that project is only ever run under a filter, and no filter had covered this. **`OpenArcsRegistry` had it in writing**, as the THIRD open item of `site_resolved_vacuum_block`: two reviewers had flagged the bridge independently, the entry said *"it may net out"* and asked for exactly this gate before anyone touched the code. It does not net out. Both are repaired here; the arc item is closed with the measured number.
- **A third thing this entry got wrong, found by a cold read after it was committed and pushed.** It cited the arc as `w1_dispersion_anchor` in three places. No such arc exists: `grep -rn "w1_dispersion_anchor" compute/` returns nothing, and the item lives under `site_resolved_vacuum_block` (`OpenArcsRegistry.cs:3819`, the settled text at `:4149`). `w1_dispersion_anchor` is a slug from the author's PRIVATE memory index, which lives outside the repo and reaches no clone. So an entry whose subject is a stale pointer sent its own reader to a store CLAUDE.md names as mandatory, under a key that store has never held. **The rule that follows: a name taken from outside the repo is not a repo name until it has been grepped inside it.** Private notes and the registry are two stores, and the private one is the one nobody else can open. Repaired 2026-08-20, after the push.
- **Lesson number two, from that: the completeness claim is the part to attack.** The blast-radius bullet above and the lesson below are both assertions about a SET (every caller, every mirror), and a set-claim is checkable in one grep by anyone who thinks to run it, which is exactly why the author does not. Writing the lesson is not the same as executing it, and the gap between the two was one line of `grep -rn "N - 1 - "` over `compute/`. And that grep is itself incomplete, which the next round showed: it finds orphans whose defect is a PRESENT reversal, never one whose defect is a MISSING one. Two of the latter exist, `KleinFourGroupSelfPairedSparseLBuilder.cs:155` and `KleinFourGroupSelfPairedRefinement.cs:234`, which took a parameter named `gammaPerSite` and indexed it by BIT. They are not orphans of `f3a58a0`, since they never compensated for anything, and their spectra are right, because the whole file is a self-consistent bit-labelled world down to its bond masks; but a caller handing over an array in Core's per-site convention would have got the profile reversed, silently, for the same reason and with the same invisibility. Repaired here by renaming the parameter to `gammaPerBit` and writing the convention out at both loops, so the name is no longer the only thing carrying it. Related shape, one entry down: *an exculpation is a claim*, and *an accusation is a claim*; now also **a sweep is a claim**.
- **Lesson, and it is about the ASSERTION rather than the arithmetic.** A commit that repairs a convention must name the consumers that MIRROR it, not count the tests that pass through it. `f3a58a0` fixed a site/bit indexing bug and checked the callers that FEED the block path; what it needed to check was the code that had copied the old convention on purpose. A deliberate mirror is exactly the thing a convention change orphans, and it is invisible to a test suite whose fixtures are symmetric under the convention in question. Corollary, cheap and general: **when a fixture is invariant under the very thing a test is meant to detect, the test is decoration.** The uniform cases here were seven green checks that could never have failed for this reason, and the repo already owned the counter-design one layer down without propagating it.

## 2026-08-19 — the F155 typed carrier: the SAME "nobody has done this" written again, one entry below the entry recording it

F155 got its typed `Claim`, its live witness, and the two primitives the Object Manager was missing. The theorem came through three fresh review rounds untouched; **every defect was in the carrier's own prose and bookkeeping**, which is now the sixth consecutive time in this corner. Two are worth the page.

- **What was wrong (1), and it is a repeat at a distance of one ledger entry:** a new test comment read *"nothing in this repo had ever passed Y in"*. `simulations/f112_klein_v4_cross_dephase_verify.py:210` passes `"Y"` into the polarity primitive and has for months. That is the same false novelty claim, about the same formula, on the same day, as item (4) of the entry directly below, an entry the same change set cites twice in other files.
- **Why it recurred, and this is the part that generalises:** the earlier entry's own lesson was written as being about a *Stage-0 record*, so the repair fixed the sweep record. This claim was not in a sweep record, it was an aside in a test comment justifying why the test was worth writing, and nothing recognised it as the same shape. **A novelty claim is a claim wherever it appears**, and "we needed this because nobody had it" is the form it takes when it is least likely to be checked, because it reads as motivation rather than as an assertion. The true and checkable statement was one word narrower: the C# implementation had no gate on the Y branch.
- **What was wrong (2), the shape the day is really about:** of roughly twenty defects the three rounds found across this change set, essentially all were introduced by the writing and the repairs rather than being present in the theorem. The most instructive: a scaling sweep offered as the tolerance "law" was **degree-0 homogeneous in the only parameter it varied**, so it could not fail; a denominator was reinstated that the ledger had retired on 2026-08-06, cited with the wrong date in four places; "six configurations" was written in five files while the set returns eight at N=2, four at N=1 and five at odd N; a `19 → 20` count was updated in the code and its test but not in `docs/PI2KB_INHERITANCE_MAP.md`; and an `IsInformative` flag let bit-exact AGREEMENT count as evidence of the ability to DISAGREE, which is circular.
- **How caught:** three fresh-agent rounds on the finished state, then a fourth pass on the repaired state, which is where (1) surfaced. Each finding was re-verified from below before being applied, and one was refuted that way: a reviewer's proposed denominator repair (‖M‖² → ‖M_anti‖²) was correct on its own grounds but did **not** cure the failure mode it was offered against, since the catastrophic-cancellation regime passes both denominators. The real repair was a second number, not a different one.
- **Fix:** the Y claim is narrowed to what is true and names the script that had already done it. The sweep is replaced by one that can fail, and the test that documents why the old sweep proved nothing is kept as a test. The denominator is `‖M_anti‖²` with a gate showing the asymmetry and that norm are both bit-identically σ-blind while `‖M‖²` moves by a factor of 37. The configuration count is pinned by `StandardSet_HasTheCountItsProseClaims` and quoted nowhere as a bare number. `IsInformative` is a three-case predicate whose middle case is stated and justified.
- **Anchor:** `docs/proofs/PROOF_F155_PHYSICAL_GENERATOR_POLARITY_BREAK.md` §(j); `compute/RCPsiSquared.Core/Symmetry/PhysicalGeneratorPolarityBreak.cs`; `compute/RCPsiSquared.Diagnostics/Foundation/PhysicalGeneratorPolarityBreakWitness.cs`.
- **Lesson.** **A "nobody has done this" is a claim, and it hides in motivation.** The previous entry filed its instance under *Stage-0 record* and repaired that surface; the same sentence walked back in through a test comment, where it read as a reason rather than as an assertion and so met no lens at all. The narrower statement is almost always both true and checkable: not "nobody has done this" but "this file has no gate on it". Second, smaller: **when a reviewer proposes the repair, the repair still has to be verified from below**: the one offered here was right about the defect and wrong about the cure, and only measuring it showed which.

## 2026-08-19 — F155 dephase siblings: a Π² grading read as a Klein index, and a "nobody has done this" written next to the file that had

The arc `polarity_break_dephase_siblings` was closed the day after it opened. The mathematics survived three fresh review rounds untouched; **every defect below is in the closing work, not in the material**, which is the fifth consecutive time that has been true in this corner of the repo.

- **What was wrong (1), and it is the older error the closure surfaced:** the typed Tier-1 claim `DissipatorAxisSelectsPolarity.cs` asserted "Π²_Y activates bit_a + bit_b simultaneously". Π_Y² grades by bit_b **alone**, the same axis as Π_Z².
- **Why:** two different maps wear the same three letters. The **Klein index of the letter** Y really is (1, 1), and that index is what F87-hardness follows. The **grading of Π_ℓ²** is bit_a for X and bit_b for Y and Z (F38/F88a). The sentence read the first as the second. The conflation was not invented in the C# file: its anchor `hypotheses/THE_POLARITY_LAYER.md` carried it in the same words, in a list explicitly of *polarity axes*, and from there it had reached `DissipatorResonanceLaw.cs` and the live arc `zeros_connecting_structure`.
- **What was wrong (2), and this one was introduced by the repair itself:** the first repair exculpated the anchor in four places, writing that it "only ever spoke of Klein cells" and "never said otherwise". It had said otherwise, in the sentence the claim was built from.
- **What was wrong (3):** the new gate script derived its closed form's support and weight **from the swap table it measured**, and its own docstring called that a safeguard against a hard-coded law masking a wrong table. It is the reverse: if Π itself is wrong, the measured table and the derived law are wrong in the matching way and the comparison succeeds on two consistently wrong quantities. Demonstrated by mutation, not feared: flipping the phase sign of the X convention in `framework.symmetry.pi_action` left the two gates whose job is to establish the law both PASSING, and only the two independent routes fired.
- **What was wrong (4):** the script's Stage-0 record claimed "no caller had ever passed a letter to the polarity primitive". `simulations/f112_klein_v4_cross_dephase_verify.py` has been passing X and Y into `polarity_coordinates_from_L` for months, and already implements the support half of the new law. That script is the verifier of the very proof the new section cites twice.
- **How caught:** three fresh-agent rounds, minimally framed, run on the finished state. A physics/mathematics round rebuilt the object from scratch in numpy and found the maths sound but caught (4) and the missing pairing caveat; a repo-consistency round found (2) plus four further copies of the conflation and a test the repair had turned red; a gate audit found (3) by mutation. Each finding was re-verified from below before being applied, and the Π-mutation result was reproduced by hand.
- **Fix:** the law's three tables are now literals in the script and the measured table is gated against them by exact set equality (both mutations are now killed by six gates each). The four exculpations were replaced by the truth and the anchor sentence itself repaired. The Stage-0 record now names the script that had already done it. The F113-under-Π_X zero is stated as what it is, a SILENCE with both ±i norms individually zero rather than a balance, and generalised to any bit_a-even content. The pairing caveat now sits on the headline formula in all three copies: the SUPPORT half is convention-free, the WEIGHT half is not.
- **Anchor:** `docs/proofs/PROOF_F155_PHYSICAL_GENERATOR_POLARITY_BREAK.md` §(g); `simulations/f155_dephase_siblings.py`; `compute/RCPsiSquared.Diagnostics/F87/DissipatorAxisSelectsPolarity.cs`; `hypotheses/THE_POLARITY_LAYER.md`.
- **Lesson, and there are two.** First: **a gate built from its own measurement cannot fail in the direction that matters.** Writing the claim down as a literal is what lets the check bite; deriving it from the thing under test only looks safer. Second: **an exculpation is a claim and needs the same check as any other.** "The anchor never said this" was written four times, in a proof, a script, a typed claim and a registry, without anyone opening the anchor; it took a fresh reader one grep. The sweep that finds the error's *copies* is not the sweep that verifies the story about where it *came from*.

## 2026-06-20 — F91/F92/F93 anti-palindromic family: "Z₄ on the parameter vector" is Klein V₄; a backwards sum/difference statement; F91's "multiset" overclaim

Second random-proof catch of the `reviewing-before-it-lands` workflow (F92 drawn by dice), with a systemic blast radius across the γ/J/h family. The core spectral results are correct and bit-exact; every defect is in the framing/decoration.

- **What was wrong (three distinct defects):**
  1. *Group structure.* F91/F92/F93 each frame the anti-palindromic parameter reshuffle R₉₀ : P ↦ 2·P_avg − F71(P) as the "90° element of a Pi2-Z₄ acting on the parameter vector, with i² = the 180° palindromic mirror F71, i⁴ = 1." On the parameter vector R₉₀ is an **involution** (R₉₀² = identity ≠ F71), so ⟨F71, R₉₀⟩ is the **Klein four-group V₄ = Z₂×Z₂**, not a cyclic Z₄; there is no order-4 element on the parameters (at N=4, dim(P_anti)=1, so no real order-4 map can exist). The genuine i⁴ = 1 / 90° quarter-turn lives only on the **operator side** (F80's Spec(M) = ±2i·Spec(H), i² = −1; `NinetyDegreeMirrorMemoryClaim`) — correct and untouched; R₉₀ is its order-2 parameter-side *shadow*.
  2. *Backwards statement.* F91 (abstract + §Pi2 prose) and F92 said R₉₀ "flips the pair-difference while preserving/fixing the pair-sum." Backwards: R₉₀ **preserves each pair-difference and reflects each pair-sum** about 2·P_avg (S ↦ 4·P_avg − S). F91's own Step 7 (Eq. 13) already had it right, so F91 internally contradicted itself (line 25/68 vs 200).
  3. *F91 content overclaim.* F91 Step 6 concluded the diagonal-block spectrum depends "only through the **multiset** of pair-sums {S_l}". It depends on the **indexed** pair-sums S_l (≙ P_sym as a vector): by F91's own Eq. 7a each diagonal element sums S_l over a specific differing-site set, so reassigning which site-pair carries which sum changes the blocks. The anti-palindromic corollary survives because there all S_l = 2·P_avg (a constant vector, where multiset = assignment).
  Plus: F92's abstract said "the eigenvalues stand still" / "the spectrum keeps the symmetric half of J" unscoped — read as the physical full-L spectrum, which moves substantially on the orbit.
- **Why:** the operator-side i⁴=1 was transported wholesale onto the parameter vector; "multiset" was a tidier-but-false weakening of what Eq. 7a proves; the abstract attached "eigenvalues" to the compressed diagonal blocks, not to L.
- **How caught:** the parallel lens panel on the dice-drawn proof — mathematical-review (Z₂-not-Z₄ + its own N=6 multiset counterexample), physics-first (full-L over-scope + the joint-popcount / F100-first-order right-sizing), survey (systemic blast radius + the F91 self-contradiction). Every headline grounded from below in `simulations/_review_f92_grounding.py` (not trusting the docs' own witnesses): R₉₀² = identity and pair-diff-preserved / pair-sum-reflected at N=4,5; R·H(J_anti)·R = −H(J_anti); diagonal-block spectra identical uniform-vs-anti-palindromic while full-L max|Δλ| ≈ 5.8 (N=4), 8.1 (N=5); and the N=6 same-multiset / different-assignment counterexample (palindromic J=(5,2,3,2,5) vs (2,5,3,5,2): same pair-sums {4,6,10}, distinct block spectra, full-L max|Δλ| = 37.8).
- **Symmetric gate:** the core (diagonal-block spectrum depends only on P_sym; invariant on the anti-palindromic orbit) is **perspectival-valid and correct** — bit-exact, NOT touched. The Z₄ label, the backwards statement, and the multiset claim are **genuine breaks** (false group, false direction, false generality), each grounded. NOT over-corrected: the 90°/Pi2 idea has a real home (operator side); the fix re-attributes, it does not delete.
- **Fix:** reframed the parameter side as Klein V₄ / shadow-of-operator-side-Z₄ across F91/F92/F93 (abstracts, tables); fixed the backwards statement; corrected F91 Step 6 + Refined-statement to indexed pair-sums (with the N=6 note); scoped F92's abstract to "diagonal-block" + added a `## What this is NOT` (full-L moves; joint-popcount is the genuine J-independent structure; F100's δλ=0 is the first-order reading); fixed the dissipator wording (Π-commuting, eigenvalue −2γ·popcount(a⊕b)); brought the three `F9xPi2Inheritance.cs`, the SpectralInvariance inspect nodes, `ANALYTICAL_FORMULAS.md`, and `SYMMETRY_FAMILY_INVENTORY.md` into line (operator-side `Pi2I4MemoryLoopClaim` left intact). Committed with this ledger entry.
- **Anchor:** `docs/proofs/PROOF_F92_BOND_ANTI_PALINDROMIC_J.md` (+ F91, F93); `simulations/_review_f92_grounding.py`.
- **Reflections flagged (Tom's hand, not auto-edited):** `reflections/ON_THE_SYMMETRY_FAMILY.md:16` ("i² = palindromic"); `reflections/ON_THE_NINETY_DEGREE_GAMMA.md:15,21` ("composition of two 90° rotations is the 180°", "(γ-distribution-90°)⁴ = identity"); and the out-of-scope `docs/carbon/POLYACETYLENE_F92_INHERITANCE.md` (Tier 2, calls the compressed sub-block a measurable spectrum) for when it graduates.
- **Lesson:** an operator-side group can be genuinely order-4 while its parameter-side shadow is only order-2 — naming the shadow by the operator's order is a false unity; check the order on the object you actually wrote the action on. And "multiset" vs "indexed tuple" is a recurring set-vs-multiset bite: state what the derivation proves, not a tidier weakening.
- **Generative pass (borrowing-a-discipline):** the P_sym/P_anti split with "the magnitude/spectrum sees only the symmetric part, the antisymmetric part lives in the phase" is term-for-term the **linear-phase FIR filter** decomposition (symmetric taps → real magnitude response; antisymmetric taps → pure phase / group delay; the Type I–IV midpoint bookkeeping = F92's middle-bond constraint). This inverts the proof's "dead-end" reading of J_anti: J_anti is a **spectrum-preserving control knob** on mode geometry / timing — the engineering question the F100 observable (c₁/Q_peak, odd in J_anti) is already circling. Candidate Tier-3 hypothesis.

## 2026-06-21 — F86a "exceptional point on the real Q axis": retracted; AND the first retraction draft over-corrected to the opposite mislabel

Third triage-drawn catch of the `reviewing-before-it-lands` workflow (F86A was triage-backlog Rank 1), and the first executed as a full subagent-driven retraction (8 tasks + 2-stage review each + a final whole-implementation review; commits `e42f64f..a0ab96d`). **Two-sided lesson: the original claim was false, AND my first retraction narrative was false in the opposite direction.**

- **What was wrong (original):** `PROOF_F86A_EP_MECHANISM.md` + the typed `LocalGlobalEpLink` (Tier2Verified) + ANALYTICAL_FORMULAS claimed the (n,n+1)-coherence block has a **defective exceptional point on the real Q axis** at Q_EP=2/g_eff, "empirically validated" by a Petermann-K sweep (K≈2384.7 at N=7, "≈6× above FRAGILE_BRIDGE's K=403", an odd/even within-parity monotonic growth law "confirming a σ_0 prediction"), connected to FRAGILE_BRIDGE's genuine complex-γ EP as "the same EP, real-axis hit confirmed".
- **What was ALSO wrong (my first retraction draft):** the initial plan replaced "defective EP on the real axis" with "the real-axis degeneracy is **diabolic** / the Petermann sweep is a **degenerate-eigenspace `eig` artifact**." Both false — in the OPPOSITE direction. The real-axis behaviour is neither defective NOR diabolic.
- **The truth (artifact-free, re-verified from below):** at the Petermann peak the relevant eigenvalues are **simple** (gap ~0.25–0.35) ⇒ no real-axis defective EP AND no diabolic degeneracy. The non-normality is **genuine, not an `eig` artifact**: the Riesz spectral-projector norm reproduces it on a SIMPLE isolated eigenvalue at Re≈−4γ₀ (N=5: ‖P‖=19.4=√375=K, gap 0.255; cond(V)=48.7/50.9/268.5 at N=5/6/7 — the opposite of diabolic's cond≈1). Only the peak MAGNITUDE/growth law is a grid artifact (K swings 2–4× over ΔQ=1e-3; the "6×", "2384.7", the parity asymmetry). Nearest complex-Q coalescences found are themselves **diabolic** (‖P‖=1) ⇒ whether the full block has an off-axis defective EP AT ALL is OPEN. Adopted reading = PT_SYMMETRY_ANALYSIS's own: "no real-axis EP; large-but-finite Petermann ⇒ a nearby EP in the complex plane." SURVIVE: t_peak=1/(4γ₀); Q_EP=2/g_eff as a DEFINITION; the toy 2×2 EP; FRAGILE_BRIDGE's separate Σγ=0 gain-loss EP (K=403); the general centered-L_c AIII chiral label; the hardware DATA.
- **How caught:** the original false claim — triage survey (F86A Rank 1) + my own from-below Petermann grid-check. The over-correction — a SECOND, sharpened physics-first review (Tom + Claude, 2nd terminal): re-derived the load-bearing number with diagnostics that never read an `eig` eigenvector (Riesz contour-integral ‖P‖ + departure-from-normality), gate-first validated on the known-defective toy 2×2 (read DEFECTIVE, dep=4.0), found the real-axis ‖P‖ large on a SIMPLE eigenvalue ⇒ genuine non-normality. Re-verified independently in the executing session (`_review_f86a_diabolic_vs_defective.py`, `_review_f86a_offaxis_ep.py`). Provenance sub-catch: the "diabolic" claim was never backed by a committed direct geometric-multiplicity/Jordan test — only `eig`-Petermann sweeps; the cited `_review_f86a_ep_check.py` was not even in the tree.
- **Symmetric gate:** original "defective EP on the real axis" = **genuine break** (no coalescence — fixed). First retraction's "diabolic / eig artifact" = **over-correction** (a fix that erased the genuine non-normality and asserted its opposite). The corrected narrative names the one object from below (a non-normal near-EP shadow) and survives the gate.
- **Fix:** full retraction across 35 files (typed claims → OpenQuestion + a typed `RetractedClaim`; PROOF_F86A §Retraction; F-registry; EP telescopes scoped to the toy; the hardware Confirmation softened to the SE-walk handover). Surviving bundle left intact + verified. Final review green: Core 63/0, Diagnostics 19/0, Runtime 54/0, Python 7/0; residual-scan clean.
- **Anchor:** `docs/proofs/PROOF_F86A_EP_MECHANISM.md` §The real-axis EP (formerly §Retraction); `LocalGlobalEpLink.cs` (OpenQuestion); `RetractedClaim` "F86a real-axis EP mechanism"; `simulations/review_f86a_diabolic_vs_defective.py` + `_review_f86a_offaxis_ep.py`; plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-06-21-f86a-ep-retraction.md`.
- **Lesson (the meta-catch):** a RETRACTION can itself over-correct — swapping one wrong label for its OPPOSITE (defective ↔ diabolic). Refuting the retracted claim is NOT enough; the REPLACEMENT narrative needs its OWN independent from-below computation. And diabolic-vs-defective-vs-near-EP cannot be separated by `eig`-Petermann alone (it inflates on near-degenerate spectra) — use the artifact-free spectral-projector norm ‖P‖, gate-first on a known toy EP (recipe: memory `reference_diabolic_vs_defective_test.md`). PENDING, deliberately NOT swept by same-method extrapolation: the **coherence-horizon √-EP** cluster (SE {0,2} Q*(N), `--root horizon`) — a preliminary SE-walk check was AMBIGUOUS, it may be a GENUINE EP — and **`F89Path3Octic`**; each needs its own grounded review.
- **Sequel (2026-07-07):** this retraction's "eigenvalues stay simple, no real-axis defective EP" was itself superseded in part — F89's nullity count proves a real-axis defective seed at every odd N, in a √-EP window ~20-30× narrower than the scan's grid. The artifact half (Petermann magnitudes) stands. Current verdict: `PROOF_F86A_EP_MECHANISM.md` §The real-axis EP.

## 2026-06-21 — coherence-horizon √-EP: CONFIRMED genuine (the contrast case to F86a — same suspect instrument, opposite-but-correct verdict)

The companion to the F86a entry above, and the case that VALIDATES the "do not refute a same-method claim by extrapolation" discipline. The F86a retraction flagged the coherence-horizon √-EP (single-excitation {0,2}-coherence, Q*(N)) as PENDING — it leans on the SAME suspect instrument (eig-based `PhaseRigidity`, r=1/√K). I deliberately did NOT refute it by extrapolation. A gate-first artifact-free re-check (Tom's request, after he laid it against the EP-sensor literature in `experiments/COHERENCE_HORIZON_EP_SENSOR_DEBATE.md`) confirmed it is **GENUINE**.

- **What was at stake:** `CoherenceHorizonClaim` (Tier1Candidate) + `THE_TRICHOTOMY_SEEN` assert the chain SE {0,2}-coherence mode undergoes a genuine 2nd-order DEFECTIVE EP at Q*(N) — but the only evidence was the raw eig-`PhaseRigidity` r→0, the family that misfired for F86a.
- **Verdict (artifact-free, confirmed):** GENUINE 2nd-order DEFECTIVE EP at N=2..5 — dep-of-compression ≈4 as the pair-split→0, geo mult 1 < alg mult 2, Schur off-diagonal = the Jordan coupling, eigenvectors merge (|cos|→1). Gate-0 (defective + diabolic toys) AND a γ=0 same-object diabolic control both pass; C# reproduces the Python bit-for-bit.
- **WHY the opposite of F86a:** it HAS the analytic discriminant double-root F86a lacked (the SE dispersion λ²+8γλ+4J²q² has a vanishing discriminant; exact at N=2,3 ⇒ Q*=1,√2). F86a's eigenvalues stayed SIMPLE (no coalescence); here they genuinely coalesce, and a 2×2 double-root with nonzero coupling is defective.
- **The instrument lesson (sharp):** the eig-`PhaseRigidity` AGREES 4/4 with the artifact-free verdict — VINDICATED — but ONLY because the object genuinely IS defective; eig-rigidity reads r→0 both on a true Jordan block AND when fooled by a clustered-but-simple spectrum (F86a). So "same suspect instrument" does NOT imply "same verdict"; only the artifact-free test distinguishes. Refusing to extrapolate-refute was right.
- **Refinements (NOT a retraction — the √-EP STANDS):** (i) the cited magnitudes (Petermann K≈7.6e4, the r→0 values 0.000/0.015/0.026, the √-exponents 0.49/0.49/0.58) are grid-sensitive — Kmax swings 5e10–6e11× over ΔQ=1e-3 (the F86a magnitude lesson); softened to "grid-sensitive certificate, not load-bearing" in THE_TRICHOTOMY_SEEN + CoherenceHorizonClaim + ANALYTICAL_FORMULAS (the EP-ness is the robust fact). (ii) recipe-refinement: ‖P‖ is trivially ≈1 on a CLOSED small block (the contour enclosing a whole 2×2) — there dep + geo/alg are the discriminators, not ‖P‖; and add a γ=0 same-object diabolic control.
- **Gap closed:** C# had NO artifact-free EP-character diagnostic (only the eig-based `PhaseRigidity`). Ported the recipe to a typed `EpCharacter` (Core/Numerics: resolvent-contour Riesz / dep / geo-alg) + a live `EpCharacterWitness` (`inspect --root epcharacter`, gate-first), breadcrumbed from `CoherenceHorizonClaim`.
- **Anchor:** `compute/RCPsiSquared.Core/Symmetry/CoherenceHorizonClaim.cs`; `EpCharacter.cs` + `EpCharacterWitness.cs` (`--root epcharacter`); `simulations/review_coherence_horizon_ep.py`; commits `d828236` (magnitude sweep) + `37f376a` (the witness). Tom anchors `experiments/COHERENCE_HORIZON_EP_SENSOR_DEBATE.md` separately (one hand per file).
- **Lesson:** the F86a meta-lesson has a TWIN — just as a retraction can over-correct into the opposite mislabel, a same-method claim must NOT be refuted by extrapolation either; the suspect instrument can be right (genuine object) or wrong (F86a), and only the artifact-free from-below test decides. STILL PENDING: `F89Path3OcticEpClaim`.

## 2026-06-21 — `F89Path3OcticEpClaim` "octic exceptional point": corrected to DIABOLIC (the third EP-character case; completes the trilogy)

The third and final EP-character case, after F86a (near-EP, retracted) and the coherence-horizon √-EP (defective, confirmed). Same suspect-instrument family, a third distinct verdict. Settled by an independent gate-first review panel, then landed via `subagent-driven-development` (Tasks 1–4 on branch `f89-octic-diabolic-correction`).

- **What was wrong:** the F89 path-3 octic degeneracy was labeled an "octic **exceptional point** / Jordan-block / defective" across the typed claim (Name/DisplayName/summary), the experiment, the F-crosswalk, the registration doc-comment, and the locator script's goal-4 ("Diagnose Jordan-block formation … rank-deficient (defective)").
- **Why it was wrong:** it is **diabolic (semisimple)** — the two octic eigenvalues coalesce (a genuine double root) but the eigenVECTORS stay independent (g1 = 2 = alg, rank(L−λ_EP·I) = n−2, dep = 0, the 2×2 restriction onto the coalescing span = scalar λ·I). **Grid-free linchpin:** disc(F_8) carries the EP-condition (3q⁴+q²−1) to **even multiplicity 2** — a *double* zero of the discriminant in q ⟹ the eigenvalue gap closes *linearly* (analytic crossing) ⟹ non-defective. A defective √-branch EP is mathematically incapable of this (it forces a *simple* zero). The octic is IRREDUCIBLE over Q(i), so the diabolicity is intrinsic to the double-zero.
- **How caught:** the diabolic-vs-defective artifact-free recipe (`reference_diabolic_vs_defective_test`), run gate-first by an independent review panel (3 agents): a fresh from-below compute with a **strengthened gate** (a Jordan block obliquely embedded in a non-normal matrix — the F86a failure mode — must still read DEFECTIVE; + a γ=0 normal control) confirmed DIABOLIC three ways (symbolic disc m=2; numerics g1=g2=2, dep=0, ‖P‖=3.88 finite; the gate). The eig-based `PhaseRigidity` was NOT trusted as evidence.
- **Fix:** re-characterized the character (NOT the values — q_EP, λ_EP, the discriminant, the AT-midpoint, Tier1Derived all stand) across the claim + a regression test (commit `ef4bf46`), the experiment + crosswalk (`3ad9414` + `519e90b`), the locator goal-4 docstring (`2847d0f`), and the registration doc-comment (`4d5aaec`).
- **Two meta-lessons (the value beyond the third data point):**
  - **A wrong character can hide inside KEPT "surviving math."** `g_eff = 2/q_EP ≈ 3.034` / "2-level effective bilinear coupling within the octic" was wrongly filed as surviving math — but it is the *F86a defective-toy-EP* signature (the inverse of F86a's EP-location relation Q_EP = 2/g_eff). The octic's own 2×2 restriction is scalar λ·I (coupling = 0), so a "bilinear coupling between two levels" would mean *defective*, contradicting the diabolic verdict. The review moved it from SURVIVES to REWORD. Lesson: sweep the *kept* list, not only the wrong list — a defective-EP descriptor can ride along in "surviving" prose.
  - **The gate fires on the reviewer's OWN appealing story.** The reviewer (me) proposed an elegant mechanism — octic = f·g with the perfect square = Res(f,g)² (two factor-blocks crossing ⟹ diabolic by construction). The from-below computation REFUTED it: the octic is irreducible (no factorisation, no resultant). Dropped, not shipped. The symmetric gate is not only for the artifact; run it on your own mechanism-stories too.
- **Execution near-misses (the per-task reviews caught what the plan-review missed):** two bare-"EP" survivors the residual-scan regex (`exceptional|defective|jordan`) does NOT match — the experiment :647 "locates **exceptional points**" (same q_EP, outside the edited subsection) and the registration :7 "octic **EP**". Both caught by the Task-1 implementer + code-quality review, folded back into the plan (Task 4 + a broadened `octic EP` regex). Also a plan-defect: the Step-10 test filter targeted `Core.Tests` for the `F89DescendantClaims` registration tests, which live in `Runtime.Tests` (the implementer ran the real ones, 16/16; command fixed). Lesson: a residual regex keyed to "exceptional/defective/jordan" silently misses the abbreviation "EP"; and a combined-filter test command can pass while matching 0 tests for one half.
- **Anchor:** `compute/RCPsiSquared.Core/Symmetry/F89Path3OcticEpClaim.cs` (+ the `Correction (2026-06-21)` para); `experiments/F89_PATH_K_GALOIS.md` § "Path-3 octic diabolic-degeneracy location" (moved 2026-07-13 from F89_TOPOLOGY_ORBIT_CLOSURE.md, the de-monolith step 2); `F_FORMULA_CROSSWALK.md:113`; commits `ef4bf46`/`3ad9414`/`519e90b`/`2847d0f`/`4d5aaec`; plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-06-21-f89-octic-diabolic-correction.md`.
- **Lesson:** the EP-character trilogy is complete and internally coherent — F86a (near-EP, no coalescence, retracted) / coherence-horizon (genuine defective EP, confirmed) / F89-octic (diabolic crossing, corrected) — all three settled by the same artifact-free apparatus (`EpCharacter` + the discriminant order-of-vanishing), and each a genuinely different outcome. The instrument earned its keep by catching a second mislabel. (Plan B — wiring the F89 diabolic verdict into the typed base — LANDED + pushed to master 2026-06-22, commits `a4e62b2`..`0dbb300`: a Core `F89Path3OcticBlock` validated as a genuine full-N=4 sub-block (max-dist 1.68e-14), a committed gate-first `EpCharacter`→DIABOLIC test, a live `inspect --root f89octic` witness, and a hardened gate (oblique-embedded + γ=0 controls); no new Claim; executed via subagent-driven-development. Follow-up ‖P‖-wording fix: ‖P‖ measures oblique embedding, NOT defective-vs-diabolic — a closed-block defective EP reads ‖P‖≈1 (Gate0a), only the eig-Petermann K diverges, which `EpCharacter` deliberately avoids.)

## 2026-06-22 — `PROOF_F101_C1_MIRROR_GAMMA_PARITY`: "depends only on γ_anti" overclaim (the γ-twin of the F100 catch), plus a self-misquote of F100 and a self-contradicting "no N-scaling"

Triage Rank 6, the γ-axis twin of F91, teed up by the F100 review (which left "F101:147 has the identical γ-overclaim — for F101's own review"). The flag was confirmed; the second lens found two more defects an F100-analogy alone would have missed. The Tier-1 core (D exactly odd in γ_anti, vanishes for palindromic γ) is correct and was confirmed from below.

- **What was wrong (three defects, all framing/accuracy):**
  1. *Overclaim (the flag).* PROOF:46 ("the c₁ bond-mirror deviation lives **entirely** in γ_anti"), PROOF:147 ("D **depends only on γ_anti** … sees γ_anti, **and sees only γ_anti**"), registry §F101 ("depends only on γ_anti, and oddly") — a functional-dependence claim contradicting the doc's OWN κ_γ section: D's *magnitude* (leading coefficient κ_γ) generically depends on γ_sym.
  2. *Self-misquote (NEW, highest-confidence).* PROOF:110 attributed "~3e−8" to F100 and put "even-power coefficients below ~3e−8" in quotation marks as F100's words — but F100:99 reports "below ~1.1e−7 (quadratic peak at N=5)"; the quoted phrase does not exist in F100. A verifiable misattribution inside quote marks, off ~3.6×. (The (0.12/0.03)²=16 arithmetic it served was itself correct.)
  3. *Self-contradiction (NEW).* PROOF:134 said κ_γ has "no N-scaling" while the doc's own :112 prints a |κ_γ| envelope that grows ~×2 per site (floor 0.55/1.39/3.05, ceiling 21.5/43.3/84.8 at N=3/4/5).
- **Why:** F101 systematically dropped the parenthetical κ-qualifier its twin F100 carries (F100:18/44/119, fixed in the F100 review); "depends only on γ_anti" conflates D's parity/zero-set (γ_anti's) with its full functional dependence (also γ_sym's). The misquote and the "no N-scaling" were independent accuracy slips, invisible to a pure F100-analogy.
- **How caught:** the F100-review hand-off flag (overclaim) + a fresh adversarial physics/math panel told the overclaim was already known and to hunt for what ELSE is wrong (it surfaced the misquote and the no-N-scaling contradiction, and confirmed the Step-1/2 algebra, the Q_peak scope obstruction, and the Tier-1 exactness are correct from below) + my OWN live run of `_f71_nonuniform_gamma_verification.py` (κ_γ swings −84.8→−40.1 as γ_sym 0.05→0.11 at fixed γ_anti; spread 75.9%; oddness + palindromic-survival reproduce at machine zero — the from-below confirmation, not the doc's table). Symmetric gate: the overclaim is a **genuine break** (D is a function of both γ_sym and γ_anti), NOT perspectival; the oddness/vanishing are correct and were preserved (no over-correction).
- **Fix:** ported F100's "its parity and zero set are fixed by γ_anti alone (its leading magnitude κ_γ also depends on γ_sym; see the κ_γ section)" qualifier into PROOF:18/46/147 and registry §F101; corrected the F100 number at PROOF:110 (both instances); reworded :134 to "no closed-form N-law (the |κ_γ| envelope grows with N…)" and swept the same shorthand into F100:133.
- **Kept (NOT over-corrected):** the zero-set/support phrasing "localises the entire bond-mirror deviation in γ_anti" (PROOF:149, inventory:208) is TRUE (D vanishes iff γ_anti=0) and parallels F100's untouched lines 121/207 — left as-is. The C# typed claim `C1MirrorGammaParity.cs` already carried the κ_γ-depends-on-γ_sym caveat and was CLEAN (the propagate-to-C# step was already done by whoever typed it). Twin-framing CLEAN: no pre-correction Z₄/order-4/"90°"/involution language in the proof or the C# claim. Private specs/plans under `docs/superpowers/` left as dated historical record.
- **Anchor:** `docs/proofs/PROOF_F101_C1_MIRROR_GAMMA_PARITY.md` (×5 edits), `docs/ANALYTICAL_FORMULAS.md` §F101, `docs/proofs/PROOF_F100_C1_QPEAK_MIRROR_J_PARITY.md` (:133 sweep); witness `simulations/f71_nonuniform_gamma_verification.py`.
- **Lesson:** a twin-of-an-already-fixed-finding review must NOT stop at porting the known fix — the second lens that hunts for "what ELSE is wrong" earned its keep here (a self-misquote of the very twin it mirrors, and a self-contradiction against its own printed table, neither reachable by analogy). And: distinguish the zero-set statement ("D vanishes off γ_anti=0", keep) from the functional-dependence statement ("D depends only on γ_anti", false) — only the latter is the overclaim.

## 2026-06-22 — `MIRROR_SYMMETRY_PROOF` (the foundational proof): the iconic "54,118 eigenvalues at N=8" is a STAR count mislabeled "chain"; plus an Ising/DM bond-type overclaim

Triage Rank 2, the single most-depended-on document in the repo (34 typed descendant claims, ~48 markdown links). The core theorem was confirmed correct from below; every defect is in the prose/labels. Reviewed via 3 agents + my own recomputes; the census fix was steered by Tom (align to the README's robust framing, don't sweep the iconic number across 25 files).

- **What was wrong (three defects; the theorem and all of Step 1-3 are correct):**
  1. *Census topology mislabel (the consequential one).* The headline census — "verified at N=8 across all **54,118** oscillatory eigenvalues **of the chain**" (lines 3/26/48), and "N=6,7,8 **chain** entries … 3,228 / 13,264 / 54,118 oscillatory" (line 286-289) — attributes STAR-topology counts to the chain. `Program.cs:111,289` run `Topology.Star`, never `Chain`; the full-dense N=6,7,8 census has always been star. The chain counts differ: 3,836 / 15,744 / 64,146.
  2. *Bond-type overclaim (line 78-82).* "The proof actually works for any sum of two-qubit Pauli bonds, including Ising and Dzyaloshinskii-Moriya: per-bond anti-commutation with Π is verified for all 16 two-qubit Pauli pairs in the explicit 16-row table below." The table is the single Heisenberg bond XX+YY+ZZ across 16 input strings; it shows neither Ising-alone nor the DM bond. **DM (XY−YX) is not palindromic under this proof's uniform Π at all** — it needs a different, site-alternating Π. "Any sum of two-qubit Pauli bonds" is false (14/36 two-term combos break, NON_HEISENBERG). This contradicted the internally-correct line 193-196.
  3. *Imprecise "oscillatory" (line 28/288).* "purely real / Im(λ) ≠ 0" — the engine actually filters `|Im| > 0.05 ∧ −Re > 0.0001`, so the "remaining 11,418 purely real" is the threshold complement, not the exactly-real count.
- **Why:** the oscillatory count is the only topology- AND threshold-dependent number in the verification (every topology has 4^N eigenvalues at size N, *all* paired — that part is invariant); the doc made the fragile number its headline and stamped "chain" on a star run. The bond-type claim transported the table's per-input-string sweep into a per-bond-type claim it does not support.
- **How caught:** triage Rank 2 → 3-agent panel (bond-type/NON_HEISENBERG; topology-reality + dependency surface; adversarial math from-below) + my OWN recomputes — per-term anti-commutation (XX/YY/ZZ each = 0.00 → Ising under the same Π; DM = 4.00 → different Π), the chain census via a block-decomposition validated exactly against the rmt CSVs (N=6=3836, N=7=15744) then run at N=8 (64,146), and reading `Program.cs` to confirm Star. The adversarial lens independently confirmed the 16-row table, the any-graph spectator-site reduction (1e-16, incl. Y/Z-spectator strings), the center=Σγ, and Π⁴=I — **the theorem stands**.
- **Symmetric gate:** the theorem and topology generality are correct (perspectival-valid, not touched); the "chain" label, the Ising/DM table claim, and the "Im≠0" wording are genuine breaks, each grounded.
- **Fix (Tom-steered framing):** reframed the headline to the README's robust, topology- and threshold-independent statement — all 65,536 eigenvalues paired at N=8, zero exceptions on every tested topology; 87,376 across N=2..8 — and demoted the oscillatory census to a correctly-attributed detail (star 3,228/13,264/54,118; chain 3,836/15,744/64,146; ring/K₄ at N=8 via block-spectrum). Rewrote line 78-82 to be NON_HEISENBERG-consistent (table = Heisenberg bond; Ising under the same Π; DM under a separate alternating Π; not "any sum"). Propagated to the C# base (`F1PalindromeIdentity.cs` docstring + verification node). **Left unswept (Tom's call):** the 25 markdown satellites citing "54,118" — it is a correct star-oscillatory count and none of them claim "chain"; the README's 87,376/all-topologies is the canonical headline and stays.
- **Anchor:** `docs/proofs/MIRROR_SYMMETRY_PROOF.md` (×6 edits); `compute/RCPsiSquared.Core/F1/F1PalindromeIdentity.cs` (×2); the README framing (verified sound). Recomputes: per-term anti-commutation + chain block-census (validated vs `simulations/results/rmt_eigenvalues_N{6,7}.csv`).
- **Lesson:** on the most-depended-on document, the *headline* number was both topology-mislabeled and built on the *fragile* (threshold-defined) quantity, while the *robust* quantity (every eigenvalue pairs, topology-independent) was sitting in the README the whole time. When a count is the iconic identity of a project, check which topology/convention actually produced it before trusting the label — and prefer the invariant framing (total-paired) over the convention-bound one (oscillatory-subset). And a "verified in the table below" citation must actually contain the object it claims to verify (the DM bond never appears in the Heisenberg table).

## 2026-06-22 — `PROOF_F86B_UNIVERSAL_SHAPE`: the "universal" two-class HWHM ratio is a class-AVERAGE that hides a 6-sub-class per-bond fan + residual c/N trends

Triage Rank 3 ("universal" resting on a polyfit over N=5–8, c∈{2,3,4}). The proof's mechanism and its derived/proven sub-results are all correct; the *headline framing* of the empirical ratio overstates an invariance that the data does not show. A classic "class-average manufactures a universality" trap.

- **What was wrong:** the Abstract + Statement 2 + summary claimed a "universal resonance shape, **two bond classes**, Interior 0.756 / Endpoint 0.770, **everything else (c, N, γ₀) drops out**", with line 243 asserting "a structural gap larger than the within-class scatter." From the raw data + the typed layer: (a) only **γ₀** drops out (bit-exact); **c and N do not** — the interior class-average climbs ~+0.014 over N=5→8 at c=3 (not converged, asymptote ≈0.79) and carries a ~0.007 c=2-vs-c≥3 offset; (b) it is **not two classes** — `BondSubClass.cs` enumerates **six** F71 orbits (Endpoint, Flanking, Mid, CentralSelfPaired, Orbit2Escape, CentralEscapeOrbit3), and the per-bond ratio spans **0.58–0.92 within a single N=8 chain** (PROOF_F90 bridge table: b3=0.578, b1/b5=0.890); (c) the inter-cluster gap (~0.006–0.007) is *smaller* than the within-class range (~0.011) — line 243 was backwards.
- **Why:** the "universal 0.756/0.770" are taken on the **F71 bond-class-AVERAGED** K(Q) curve — `C2HwhmRatio.cs` admits in code "average K(Q) curves first, then extract peak … the class-averaged curve smooths over this." The averaging over heterogeneous bonds (0.58–0.92) is what creates the apparent two-valued universality; per bond it isn't there. And the doc's own closed form `HWHM = 0.671535 + α_subclass·g_eff + β_subclass` is explicitly g_eff(N,b)-dependent — i.e. the proof simultaneously claims "N drops out" (headline) and "depends on g_eff" (closed form).
- **How caught:** triage Rank 3 → 2-agent panel (empirical attack reconstructing the per-bond ratios from the raw `eq022_*/summary.json` + the bridge table; F86-family/tier-consistency + independent F88b math verification) + my own reading of `BondSubClass.cs` (6 orbits) and the PROOF_F90 per-bond table (0.58–0.92). The math-verification lens separately confirmed the embedded F88b derivation is entirely correct (Krawtchouk lemma, the 4-line α=(1−γ²)/2 Dicke proof, all 12 table rows, bit-exact) and the bare floor 0.671535 is genuinely derived (transcendental, reproduced to 1e-3 from the analytic K_b(x) forms) — so the catch is confined to the empirical-ratio framing.
- **Symmetric gate:** the relative-Q lineshape collapse (the x-table collapses to ~1%), γ₀-invariance, the derived floor, and F88b are all correct (perspectival-valid, kept and foregrounded). The "universal / everything drops out / two classes / gap > scatter" claims are genuine breaks against the data (grounded from below), fixed.
- **Fix:** reframed Abstract + Statement 2 + the "What this tells us" summary to: γ₀-invariant **relative-Q lineshape collapse** is the robust Tier-1-candidate finding; the HWHM ratio is a **coarse ~2% class-average** carrying residual c/N trends; per bond it resolves into the **six** `BondSubClass` orbits (0.58–0.92). Swept the identical overclaim in `ANALYTICAL_FORMULAS.md` §F86b (the ratio block, the "gap larger than within-class spread" sentence, the "named structural law" restatement). **No C# fix needed — the typed base was already honest** (6 sub-classes, the averaging caveat in code, Tier1Candidate labels); the markdown had drifted *away* from the more-honest base.
- **Anchor:** `docs/proofs/PROOF_F86B_UNIVERSAL_SHAPE.md` (×10 edits); `docs/ANALYTICAL_FORMULAS.md` §F86b (×3); grounding in `compute/RCPsiSquared.Core/F86/Item1Derivation/BondSubClass.cs`, `C2HwhmRatio.cs`, `docs/proofs/PROOF_F90_F86C2_BRIDGE.md` per-bond table. Left as coarse downstream values (not swept): the terse hub/inventory one-liners + the water/carbon application docs citing 0.756/0.770 as approximate predictions.
- **Lesson:** when a "universal constant" is computed on a **class-averaged** observable, check the per-element spread before calling it universal — averaging over a heterogeneous family (here 0.58–0.92) manufactures an invariance that no member has. And a headline "everything drops out" that coexists with an in-doc closed form depending on g_eff(N,b) is self-contradicting: the parameter the closed form needs is exactly the one the headline says dropped out. (Counterpart to the MIRROR_SYMMETRY census catch the same day: there the robust quantity was hiding in the README; here the honest structure was hiding in the typed C# the markdown drifted from.)

## 2026-06-22 — `PROOF_F80_BLOCH_SIGNWALK`: "generalizes verbatim" to k-body + "including K_N (see below)" — two scope overstatements on an otherwise-correct proof

Triage Rank 4 (scope + set/multiset). A *light* catch: the proof is careful and largely pre-empts the triage flags (it states the multiset equality with explicit ×2^N multiplicity, and cleanly separates the general structural identity from the open k-body cluster-values). The mathematical core is correct, verified bit-exact from below. Two presentational scope overstatements remained.

- **What was wrong:** (1) the k-body extension (lines 68, 73) said "Steps 5/6-7 **generalize verbatim**", but Step 5's flip argument (line 180) rests on "a Π²-odd bond carries **exactly one X**", which is 2-body-specific and fails for k≥3 ((X,X,Y) has 2, (Y,Y,Y) has 0). (2) the abstract (line 27) claimed the identity "extends to all topologies, **including the complete graph K_N (see below)**", but K_N appears in no "below" section and no test, and an ordered (P,Q) bond on a non-path edge has an orientation ambiguity (H(i→j) ≠ H(j→i)) the doc never fixes.
- **Why it's only presentational:** the k-body identity Spec(M)=±2i·Spec(H) *is* correct (the flip just needs the general factor (−1)^{n_Y+n_Z}, = −1 for any Π²-odd term, with sign s=(−1)^{n_Y+1} = −∏ε_{P_i}); and the K_N identity *does* hold (spot-checked at K₄ from below). The overstatements were the word "verbatim" hiding a needed restatement, and a forward-reference "(see below)" that was never delivered.
- **How caught:** triage Rank 4 → one adversarial math lens that built Π/L/M in numpy from scratch and (a) verified the core bit-exact (Step-5 identity err=0, Spec(M)=±2i·Spec(H) mult×2^N, M literally −2i·(I⊗H), ring two-sector at N=4/6), (b) refuted "verbatim" by exhibiting the >1-X and 0-X Π²-odd k-body terms and deriving the general (−1)^{n_Y+n_Z} flip, (c) confirmed K_N is verified nowhere and exhibited the orientation asymmetry. I had reached the same two nits on my own read; the lens grounded them.
- **Symmetric gate:** the theorem, the Step-5 mechanism, the multiset identity, and the topology/body-count *generality* are all correct (kept). "verbatim" and "K_N (see below)" are genuine (if small) breaks against what the proof actually delivers — fixed.
- **Fix:** stated the general flip factor (−1)^{n_Y+n_Z} / s=(−1)^{n_Y+1} at the k-body extension (lines 68, 73); scoped the K_N claim honestly (line 27: identity holds at K₄ by the per-bond lemma, but K_N is not in the cluster-value table and its ordered bonds need an orientation convention). Swept the twin "verbatim" in `ANALYTICAL_FORMULAS.md` §F85's F-chain table. The F-registry §F80 was already honest ("Still open: … the complete graph K_N"), and the typed C# (`BlochSignWalk.cs`, `F80ExtensionExplorationTests`) covers only chain/ring/star/3-/4-body with no K_N claim — no C# change.
- **Anchor:** `docs/proofs/PROOF_F80_BLOCH_SIGNWALK.md` (×3); `docs/ANALYTICAL_FORMULAS.md` §F85 (×1).
- **Lesson:** "generalizes verbatim" is worth a gate every time — a per-bond/per-site argument written for the 2-body case often needs a small restatement (here the X-count premise → the parity factor (−1)^{n_Y+n_Z}) even when the *result* extends cleanly. And a "(see below)" that promises a case (K_N) the body never delivers is a citation that doesn't contain its object (the same shape as the MIRROR_SYMMETRY "verified in the table below" catch).

## 2026-06-22 — `PROOF_F110` / `PROOF_F111`: a Tier1Candidate→Tier1Derived promotion that updated the headers but not the bodies — and the stale tier label had propagated across the whole y_par sibling family (~16 files)

Triage Rank 7 (status churn, "cheap to verify+fix"). The verify WAS cheap (the typed base is the arbiter); the fix had a wide blast radius — a 2026-06-10 promotion that never propagated.

- **What was wrong:** F110 and F111 (both `Tier.Tier1Derived` in their binding C# `base()` ctors since 2026-06-10) carried self-contradicting prose: the headers + §5 said Tier1Derived / converse closed, while the bodies said "Tier 1 candidate" (F111:3/22), "what remains for full Tier1Derived is the hard-direction converse" (F111:138/152), "F111 is Tier1Candidate (not Tier1Derived)" (F110:80/100), and Aspects B/C "empirical" (F110:20/30/35/62). The same stale "F110 (HardCellYInversionPattern, Tier1Candidate)" cross-reference had propagated to every y_par sibling written 2026-05-25 (pre-promotion): 5 sibling proofs (F107, F108 Parts 1/2/3, F109), their 5 typed-claim inspect summaries, the F-registry (2 spots), SYMMETRY_FAMILY_INVENTORY (2 rows), the F110 typed docstring (Aspect-B "empirical"), and a Runtime test comment — ~16 files.
- **Why:** the tier PROMOTION (2026-06-10, when the windowed hard-direction converse closed via WindowedConverseAllGammaClaim / Pascal-Gram positivity F117) updated the two primary claim headers + the typed `base()` Tier but never swept the body prose or the sibling cross-references naming F110/F111's tier.
- **How caught:** triage Rank 7 → read the binding tier from the typed base (HardCellYInversionPattern, HardCellPureDTemplate both `Tier.Tier1Derived`; WindowedConverseAllGammaClaim `Tier.Tier1Derived`, "no residual"), the arbiter the "C# is the base" discipline names. The converse closure is genuine (typed Tier1Derived + PascalGramPositivityWitness recomputes the F117 coefficients live to exact CRT integers), so the promotion is real and the reconciliation is UP (stale prose → Tier1Derived), not down.
- **Symmetric gate:** the typed base + headers (Tier1Derived) are correct (kept); the body/sibling "Tier1Candidate / converse open / empirical" labels are genuine breaks against the typed truth (swept). NOT over-corrected: preserved subclaim (d)'s real "closed modulo M = −2i(H⊗I)" caveat (a bit-exact one-sidedness, not a gap), the genuinely-open items (k≥5 unverified, hardware QPU k≥3, Pure-D Rule scope k>4), and the correct historical narration ("Originally/shipped Tier1Candidate … promoted 2026-06-10").
- **Fix:** reconciled F110 (7 spots) + F111 (4 spots) to Tier1Derived/converse-closed; swept the "F110 Tier1Candidate" cross-reference across the 5 sibling proofs + 5 sibling typed inspect summaries + F-registry (2) + inventory (2) + the F110 docstring + 1 test comment (mechanical literal-replace, then verified). C# build + Core.Tests 169/169 + Runtime.Tests 7/7 green — no test asserted the old summary strings.
- **Anchor:** `docs/proofs/PROOF_F110_HARD_CELL_Y_INVERSION.md` + `PROOF_F111_HARD_CELL_PURE_D_TEMPLATE.md`; `docs/ANALYTICAL_FORMULAS.md` §F110/§F111; `docs/SYMMETRY_FAMILY_INVENTORY.md`; the 5 sibling proofs + 5 sibling typed claims (F107/F108×3/F109); `compute/.../Symmetry/HardCellYInversionPattern.cs`; the typed arbiter `compute/.../F87/WindowedConverseAllGammaClaim.cs`.
- **Lesson:** a tier promotion is a SWEEP, not a one-line header edit — the old tier label lives in the claim's own body AND in every sibling's "see also" cross-reference (here ~16 files), and the binding truth is the typed `base()` Tier, not any prose. When reconciling status churn, read the typed base first to fix the DIRECTION (here UP — the prose lagged a real promotion), then grep the tell-tale "ClaimName, OldTier" string repo-wide.

## 2026-06-22 — `PROOF_F113`: a triage "tiny-N overclaim" that REVERSED from below — the claim is genuinely general-N, the proof just cited the wrong sibling for one step

Triage Rank 8 flagged F113's "for any N≥1" closed form as anchored only at N=2,3,4 and leaning on an N≤4 F112 input. The flag was sharp and correct to fire — but the from-below check reversed the conclusion: the claim is genuinely Tier1Derived general-N; the proof's prose just mis-cited one step.

- **What the flag suspected:** F113's reduction (line 51-56) drops the T1-self-term ‖L_T1,+i‖²−‖L_T1,-i‖² citing "= 0 by F112 non-Hermitian extension at N≤4" (line 53), and line 391 called that extension "Tier1Derived at N≤4, Tier1Candidate at N≥5" — so "Tier1Derived for general N" looked like it rested on a tiny-N input.
- **What was actually true (from below):** (1) the closed form holds bit-exact at N=5 AND N=6 (residual ~1e-13/1e-16); (2) the T1-self-term vanishes bit-exact at N=5/6; (3) **decisively, the self-term is general-N-closable by F113's OWN per-site machinery** — the single-site Π²-odd part of L_T1 is the lone real (Z,I) entry whose ±i projections are equal-norm (shifted-diagonal support ⟹ ⟨e,ΠeΠ⁻¹⟩=0, Π unitary), and cross-site terms vanish by the same Tr((L_T1,1)_±i)=0 the proof already uses for the cross term; tensor factorization lifts both to all N. So the N≤4 citation is a NON-BINDING convenience, not a load-bearing input. (4) The cited "F112 non-Hermitian extension" is itself Tier1Derived universal-N now (Welle 11) AND concerns the commutator −i[H,·], not the dissipator self-term — so line 53 pointed at the wrong sibling object for a conclusion F113 establishes itself.
- **Why it's prose-lag, not overclaim:** the typed base already asserts the strong claim independently — F113 ctor `Tier.Tier1Derived` general-N (Welle 4), F112 ctor `Tier.Tier1Derived` universal-N (Welle 11). The proof's committed Lemma-C closure runs to N=5. So "Tier1Derived general-N" is the typed truth; the body prose + the F113 docstring + the crosswalk lagged it.
- **How caught:** triage Rank 8 → a from-below lens (built M at N=5/6, computed the formula + self-term, traced the self-term's general-N closability, checked the typed `base()` tiers) + my own read of the F113 ctor (Tier1Derived general-N, contradicted by its own stale docstring at lines 52-57 "open / would promote").
- **Symmetric gate:** the typed base + N=5/6 numerics + the self-contained self-term argument all support Tier1Derived general-N (kept, NOT downgraded). The misleading citation + the stale "open/candidate" labels are genuine breaks against that truth (fixed). Preserved the genuine empirical-confirmation framing (the original sweep was N=2,3,4; now bit-exact N=2..6).
- **Fix:** replaced PROOF:53's wrong-sibling citation with a self-contained T1-self-term note (the proof's own Steps-4-6 machinery); corrected PROOF:391's stale F112-extension tier; fixed the F113 typed docstring (52-57, "open/would promote" → Tier1Derived general-N) and F_FORMULA_CROSSWALK (F113 "Tier1Candidate general N" → Tier1Derived; F112 "non-Hermitian Tier1Candidate empirical only" → Tier1Derived universal-N).
- **Anchor:** `docs/proofs/PROOF_F113_COEFFICIENT_DERIVATION.md`; `compute/RCPsiSquared.Core/Symmetry/LindbladBitBPiBreakMagnitude.cs` (ctor Tier1Derived; docstring fixed); `compute/RCPsiSquared.Core/F_FORMULA_CROSSWALK.md`; the typed arbiter pair F113/F112 ctors.
- **Lesson:** a "tiny-N overclaim" flag can reverse — the right move is to ask whether the proof's OWN machinery already closes the general-N step, and to read the binding typed `base()` Tier, before concluding overclaim. Here the claim was sound; the defect was a citation pointing at the wrong sibling (commutator extension vs dissipator self-term) plus a docstring that contradicted its own constructor. Distinguish "the conclusion is unproven" (downgrade) from "the conclusion is proven but mis-cited" (fix the citation, keep the tier).

## 2026-06-22 — `PROOF_F90_F86C2_BRIDGE`: core unity is EXACT (stronger than stated), but four documentation defects — a backwards convention statement, a superseded lift mechanism, stale "universal" wording, a reproducibility gap

Triage Rank 10 flagged the "same object" unity + the "20/22 bonds + orbit escapes" seam. The from-below verdict: the unity is **genuine and operator-exact** — and the seam is **perspectival**. The catches are all documentation, none touch the Tier1Derived identity (in fact it was strengthened).

- **Core verdict (GO):** the F86 c=2 (1,2)-coherence-block Liouvillian and the F89 path-(N−1) (SE,DE) sub-block are **bit-identical operators**: `‖L_F86(J) − L_F89(J/2)‖_F = 0` exactly (integer-coefficient matrices) at N=5,6,7,8. Confirmed by my own from-scratch build at N=5/6 AND an independent reviewer build at N=5..8. So the "20/22 bit-exact, 2/22 within-noise" seam is a pure Q-grid-sampling artifact (PERSPECTIVAL): on a shared grid the N=8 b=2/b=4 residual is 4.3e-15, and my own fine-grid run gives b=2=b=4=0.74835 (one value; the 0.0008 is the gap between two coarse grids). Orbit escapes are real high-Q resonances, identical on both sides. There cannot be a break — there is one operator.
- **Finding D (genuine break, both markdown + C#): a backwards convention statement.** PROOF:42 said "Hence F89's J = 2·F86's J, equivalently Q_F89 = Q_F86/2" — internally contradictory (J_F89=2·J_F86 ⟹ Q_F89=2·Q_F86). The C# docstring (`F90F86C2BridgeIdentity.cs` :24, :59-61) carried the same flip ("F89's J equals JConventionFactor × F86's J, hence Q_F89 = Q_F86/JConventionFactor"), and the ambiguous shorthand "modulo F89-J = 2·F86-J convention" propagated to the base description, `F_FORMULA_CROSSWALK`, `F86KnowledgeBase`, `ANALYTICAL_FORMULAS`. **The operative quantities were all correct** (`F86JToF89J = f86J/2`, `Q_F89 = Q_F86/2`, the table F89 Q≈1.27 vs F86≈2.5) — only the English sentence was reversed.
